## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck makes one argument in a clear order: hype and money (3-10), then 'what is this?' (13-36), then value capture and platform shifts (37-47), then AGI and uncertainty (61-72). Each slide carries one idea in a consistent system of a serif title, a one-line subtitle, a red accent for the point of interest and grey for context. Charts such as slide 3 are clean and legible, and the S-curve built across slides 16-19 and the strikethrough on slide 30 show the thinking well. The weaknesses are tiny legend and axis text on the chart slides (4, 5, 8-10), thin pages with big empty areas (2, 11, 28, 35), and a template that gets repetitive, with the circle-and-arrow diagrams recurring (13, 21, 34, 39, 47, 60, 64). Several pages also depend on the speaker to carry the meaning.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the single-idea slides, the red-versus-grey emphasis, the S-curve build on slides 16-19, and the strikethrough reveal on slide 30.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge legends and axis labels on the chart slides (4, 5, 8, 9, 10) and give the circle diagrams more varied or more informative forms.

**What would change the score:** Shrink the empty white boxes on the quote slides (2, 11, 35, 58) and the section dividers (14, 48, 62), and put the Barksdale quote on slide 40 only once, not again on slide 53.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 87 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a Bill Gates quote placing ChatGPT alongside the GUI as a revolutionary technology. It then shows the hype around AI through Hacker News trends, Y Combinator startup fields, venture investment, and a wave of new models from OpenAI, Google, Meta and open source projects. Next it turns to compute demand, with Nvidia revenue and hyperscaler capex charts, and to corporate adoption. The middle sections argue that generative AI is a platform shift, using S-curves and past platforms, then question whether it is a database, a reasoning engine or something else, and examine how value is bundled and unbundled. Later sections cover the path toward AGI and the uncertainty about what it means. The closing sections place AI within wider trends such as regulation, e-commerce, online dating and fast fashion, and end with a short About slide and a thank-you page.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans AI, and everything else deck have?**

The deck has 87 slides. Slide 1 is the title slide, which reads "AI, and everything else" by Benedict Evans, December 2023.

**What does the Benedict Evans AI deck say about Nvidia revenue?**

Slide 8, titled "Nvidia can't keep up", says there is a rush to buy GPU compute capacity at 40% operating profit. It charts Nvidia quarterly revenue by segment in $bn, with the data centre and gaming and other segments, sourced from Nvidia.

**What does the Benedict Evans AI deck say about hyperscaler spending on AI infrastructure?**

Slide 9 says the "big three" cloud platforms may spend $100bn on infrastructure this year. The chart shows platform capex in $bn for Meta, Alphabet, AWS and Microsoft from 2015 to 2023e.

**What does the Benedict Evans AI deck say about ChatGPT users and OpenAI revenue?**

Slide 11 states "100m ChatGPT weekly active users!" and "OpenAI has $1.3bn revenue!!". The slide carries no further detail on these figures.

**Layout**

Single chart slide on a light grey header and footer band with a white content panel between them. The title sits top-left at x 20.5% and the subtitle sits directly beneath it, both left-aligned on the same left edge. The chart runs from about x 18% to x 95.5%, with the legend outside it at the far left (x 4.8%). The footer carries a left-aligned source line at x 20.5% and a right-aligned author, date and page number group ending at x 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart titled 'Y Combinator startups by field'. Y axis runs 0 to 500 with labelled ticks at 0, 100, 200, 300, 400 and 500, no unit label. Dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at each tick. X axis has 18 thin bars, one per half-period, with labels W 2015, W 2016, W 2017, W 2018, W 2019, W 2020, W 2021, W 2022 and W 2023 placed under alternate bars. Two stacked series: bottom series 'AI' in red, top series 'Other' in dark grey. Each bar has a thin white separator between the segments. Values are not printed; estimates from bar heights (about): bar 1 total about 110, AI about 15; bar 2 total about 105, AI about 5; bar 3 total about 122, AI about 15; bar 4 total about 101, AI about 23; bar 5 total about 116, AI about 20; bar 6 total about 126, AI about 23; bar 7 total about 148, AI about 24; bar 8 total about 131, AI about 19; bar 9 total about 196, AI about 24; bar 10 total about 173, AI about 24; bar 11 total about 227, AI about 34; bar 12 total about 208, AI about 24; bar 13 total about 336, AI about 49; bar 14 total about 393, AI about 69; bar 15 total about 401, AI about 66; bar 16 total about 234, AI about 52; bar 17 total about 270, AI about 84; bar 18 total about 217, AI about 138. Legend at left, outside the plot: dark grey square 'Other' above red square 'AI'. No highlighted points. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF content panel between y 20.5% and y 86.1%. Title and subtitle colour #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight, with title roughly 5.6% of slide height and subtitle roughly 2.6%. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold, sans-serif, #545554, about 2.2%. Axis labels, legend and footer text in MessinaSans-Regular, sans-serif, about 1.85% of slide height. Author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6; date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Accent red #E84C3D for AI series; dark grey #535353 for Other series. Gridlines are dotted, about 0.5 px, light grey #BFBFBF. Category axis baseline is a thin solid grey line #8C8C8C. Bars are flat, square-cornered, no shadows or gradients, with white 1 px separators between segments.

**Layout**

Single-column slide with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge, starting at the top, with the title at about 5.6% from the top. Four equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the vertical middle (y 39.1% to 67.9%), spaced 20% apart, with grey arrows between circles 2 and 3 and circles 3 and 4 and an emoji between circles 1 and 2; the left margin under the subtitle is empty. The footer sits bottom right at about 91% from the top, with the author name, date and page number 6 on one line, right of centre; there is no logo.

**Visuals**

A process diagram with four circles in a row and connectors. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 with the text 'OpenAI! / (& Microsoft)'. Between circle 1 and circle 2 is a colour emoji of a face with exploding head (🤯, a head bursting into a mushroom-cloud shape with orange-yellow debris, face shown with an open-mouthed worried expression), about 3.5% wide by 6.2% high, placed on the background with no box. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 with the text 'Google & Meta / rush to ship / their own'. Between circle 2 and circle 3 is a solid right-pointing block arrow in light grey #A6A6A6, about 4.0% wide by 7.0% high, vertically centred on the circles. Circle 3 is dark grey #535353 with the text 'New / entrants & / open source / boom'. Between circle 3 and circle 4 is a second identical grey block arrow. Circle 4 is red #EB483F, the only accent-coloured shape, with the text 'Lots of / models / Lots of / questions'. No chart, table or photograph. Nothing is cropped, shadowed or gradient-filled; all shapes are flat.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the whole slide, with no gradient, photograph or pattern. Flat, minimal look on a light grey background. Titles and subtitle are dark grey #545454 in a transitional serif (MessinaModern-Regular, Didone-like with bracketed serifs), regular weight. Title cap height is about 5.6% of slide height (font size 5.56%); subtitle is about 2.6% of slide height, the same serif. Text in circles is white #FFFFFF in MessinaModern-Book, regular weight, about 3.0% of slide height, sentence case, centred. Footer is light grey #A6A6A6 in MessinaSans, a humanist sans-serif: 'Benedict Evans' in Bold, the rest in Book, about 1.85% of slide height. Accent colours: red #EB483F on the final circle; neutral grey #535353 circles; arrows #A6A6A6. Circles are perfect circles with no outline, no shadow, no corner radius because they are circles; arrows have no outline.

### Slide 7

**Text**

Lots of science questions…
OpenAI broke through by making a giant model, so now, do models get still-bigger, or smaller?
Few models?
A few huge, powerful, / capital-intensive / models relied on for / everything else
How many / models, how / big, how / powerful, how / expensive?
Many models?
Many models of / different sizes, / costs & capabilities
(c.f. databases)
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 7

**Layout**

Single slide with a left-aligned serif title at the top left (x about 20.5%, y about 5.6%) and a one-line serif subtitle beneath it, both aligned to the same left edge. A horizontal row of three circles is centred in the middle band of the slide (y about 34% to 73%), with two grey block arrows between them pointing outward from the centre: left arrow points left, right arrow points right. Footer sits at the bottom right (y about 91%): bold 'Benedict Evans', then '–– December 2023', then the page number 7 at x about 95%. Margins are roughly 20% left and 5% right for the title block.

**Visuals**

Three circles and two block arrows, all vector shapes, no photographs, charts, logos or icons. Left circle: white fill, solid dark grey outline (#545454, about 2 px at slide scale), containing a bold heading 'Few models?' and body text. Centre circle: no visible fill (background shows through), dotted dark grey outline (#545454, about 2 px dots), containing bold text 'How many models, how big, how powerful, how expensive?'. Right circle: white fill, solid red outline (about #E74C3C, about 2 px), containing bold heading 'Many models?' and body text. Two block arrows in mid grey #A7A7A7 with flat fill and no outline: the left one (x about 41.6%, w 5.2%, h 9.3%) is a left-pointing block arrow with a shaft and head; the right one (x about 69.0%) is a right-pointing block arrow of the same size. Arrows point away from the centre circle, toward the left and right circles. No shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look. Background light grey #F1F1F1. Title and body text dark grey #545454. Footer text light grey #A6A6A6. Accent colours: red outline #E74C3C on the right circle only; arrows #A7A7A7. Typefaces: a transitional serif (Messina Modern family) for title, subtitle and all circle text, in Regular/Book and SemiBold weights; a sans-serif (Messina Sans) for the footer, Bold for the name and Book for the date and number. Title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle headings about 3.3%, circle body about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. Circles have perfectly round shapes with no corner radius, shadow or gradient.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A two-colour stacked bar chart with a minimal title and subtitle keeps the data legible and the message immediate.

**Text**

Nvidia can’t keep up…
The rush to buy GPU compute capacity (at 40% operating profit)
Nvidia quarterly revenue by segment ($bn)
Data centre
20
Gaming & other
15
10
5
0
Mar-15
Mar-16
Mar-17
Mar-18
Mar-19
Mar-20
Mar-21
Mar-22
Mar-23
Source: Nvidia
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
8

**Layout**

A full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band runs across the top 20% of the slide, holding the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.5%, which sets a left text edge shared by the chart title and source line. Below it a white panel fills the middle band from y 20.5% to 86%, containing a legend at the far left (x 4.8%) and a single stacked column chart spanning about x 18.5% to 95% of the width. A light grey footer band below y 86% holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number right-aligned at the bottom-right margin.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled 'Nvidia quarterly revenue by segment ($bn)'. Vertical axis labelled in $bn with tick labels 0, 5, 10, 15, 20 and a range of 0 to 20; horizontal dotted gridlines at each tick in light grey. Horizontal axis shows quarterly periods labelled Mar-15, Mar-16, Mar-17, Mar-18, Mar-19, Mar-20, Mar-21, Mar-22 and Mar-23 at every fourth bar, with about 35 quarterly columns in total. Two series stacked per column: bottom series 'Gaming & other' in dark grey, top series 'Data centre' in red. Values are not printed; estimates: first column about 1.2 total (about 0.9 gaming, 0.3 data centre); Mar-20 column about 3.1 total; Mar-22 peak column about 8.2 total (about 4.5 gaming, 3.7 data centre); the penultimate tall column about 13.5 total (about 3.2 gaming, 10.3 data centre); the final column is the tallest at about 18.1 total (about 3.6 gaming, 14.5 data centre). Legend at left: red square labelled 'Data centre' above dark grey square labelled 'Gaming & other'. No highlighted points beyond the tallest final column.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart panel. Title and subtitle colour #545454 in a high-contrast serif (Modern serif, similar to Messina Modern Regular). Chart title, axis labels, legend and source line in #545454 / #535353 in a geometric sans-serif (Messina Sans, regular weight; chart title semibold). Footer author name bold #A6A6A6, date and page number book weight #A6A6A6. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and axis text about 1.9%. Accent colours: data centre series red #E84A3C; gaming series dark grey #545454. Bars are flat with no outlines, thin white separation at segment boundaries; gridlines are 0.5px dotted #B0B0B0. No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 9

**Text**

And the hyperscalers… scale / The ‘big three’ cloud platforms may spend $100bn on infrastructure this year / Platform capex* ($bn) / Meta / Alphabet / AWS / Microsoft / 150 / 125 / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023e / Source: Companies, Goldman Sachs / *Including capital leases / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 9

**Layout**

Full-slide light grey (#F1F1F1) field with a white band across the middle (from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height) holding a left-aligned title block, the chart and the footer; all text starts at about x 20.5%, which is a left margin of roughly one fifth of the slide width. The title sits at the top of the grey header area, the subtitle directly beneath it, the chart title just above the plot, the legend at the far left of the chart area, and the source note bottom left with the author, date and page number bottom right on the grey footer strip; the page number '9' sits at the far right edge (about x 94.9%).

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart, titled 'Platform capex* ($bn)'. Vertical axis: 'Platform capex* ($bn)' from 0 to 150 in steps of 25, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 25, 50, 75, 100, 125 and 150; no axis title beyond the chart title. Horizontal axis: categories 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023e (the last marked as an estimate). Four stacked series, bottom to top: Microsoft (red #EE4B3F), AWS (salmon #F4A09B), Alphabet (pale pink #F9DAD7), Meta (dark grey #505050); each segment is separated by a thin white line. No data labels are printed, so all values are estimates: 2015 about Microsoft 7, AWS 3, Alphabet 10, Meta 2 (total about 22); 2016 about Microsoft 10, AWS 5, Alphabet 10, Meta 4 (total about 29); 2017 about Microsoft 11, AWS 7, Alphabet 13, Meta 7 (total about 38); 2018 about Microsoft 16, AWS 9, Alphabet 26, Meta 13 (total about 64); 2019 about Microsoft 18, AWS 13, Alphabet 24, Meta 15 (total about 69); 2020 about Microsoft 21, AWS 17, Alphabet 22, Meta 15 (total about 75); 2021 about Microsoft 27, AWS 23, Alphabet 25, Meta 19 (total about 94); 2022 about Microsoft 28, AWS 30, Alphabet 32, Meta 31 (total about 121); 2023e about Microsoft 40, AWS 32, Alphabet 32, Meta 29 (total about 133). Legend at the left of the chart, listed top to bottom: Meta (dark grey square), Alphabet (pale pink square), AWS (salmon square), Microsoft (red square). No highlighted points. Bars are of equal width, about 6% of the plot width each, with the plot baseline a thin grey line at 0.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 with a central white band #FFFFFF; no gradients, shadows or rules. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern/Didone-style serif), regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height for the title and 2.6% for the subtitle, also #545454. Chart title and all axis, legend and footer text in a geometric grotesque sans (MessinaSans family): chart title SemiBold #545554 at 2.2%, axis and legend labels Regular #545454 / #535353 at 1.85%, footer 'Benedict Evans' Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number Book #A6A6A6, all 1.85%. Bars use flat fills in a red-to-pink ramp with white separators; gridlines are thin dotted light grey; no corner radii.

### Slide 10

**Text**

“What’s our AI strategy?” / Huge interest in old economy companies after just a few months / Corporate management exposure to generative AI, April 2023 / Have not tried / 100% / Have tried / Use for personal / Use for work & personal / Use for work / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / TMT / Finance / High-tech / Healthcare & pharma Energy & materials / Professional / Consumer / services / Source: McKinsey / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 10

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands frame the slide: a light grey #F1F1F1 header band (top about 0 to 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, and a white content area (about 20.5% to 86% of height) holding the chart. A grey #F1F1F1 footer strip lies below the white area and holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at about x 20.5%, the chart title and plot share that edge, and the legend sits at the far left at about x 4.8%. The margins are wide on the left (about 20%) and narrow on the right (about 5%). Slide number 10 sits at bottom right at about x 94.5%.

**Visuals**

Chart: 100% stacked column chart, vertical bars. Title: Corporate management exposure to generative AI, April 2023. Y axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 100%, labelled at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. X axis: seven categories, labelled TMT, Finance, High-tech, Healthcare & pharma, Energy & materials, Professional services, Consumer. Series, bottom to top: Use for work (black #000000), Use for work & personal (dark grey #545454), Use for personal (mid grey #808080), Have tried (light grey #A6A6A6), Have not tried (red #EA4A41). No values are printed on the bars; the following are estimates marked about. TMT: Use for work about 15%, work and personal about 20%, personal about 17%, have tried about 39%, have not tried about 9.5%. Finance: work about 8.5%, work and personal about 17%, personal about 18%, have tried about 42%, have not tried about 14%. High-tech: work about 5.5%, work and personal about 12%, personal about 17%, have tried about 50%, have not tried about 16%. Healthcare and pharma: work about 6.5%, work and personal about 11%, personal about 18%, have tried about 47%, have not tried about 16%. Energy and materials: work about 6%, work and personal about 9%, personal about 15%, have tried about 51%, have not tried about 19%. Professional services: work about 7.5%, work and personal about 16%, personal about 13%, have tried about 42%, have not tried about 21%. Consumer: work about 7.5%, work and personal about 12%, personal about 12%, have tried about 42%, have not tried about 27%. Segments are separated by thin white hairlines. Bars are about 4.3% of slide width wide with equal spacing of about 10.7%. Legend: five square swatches, about 1.5% of slide width each, stacked vertically at left, top to bottom: Have not tried (red), Have tried (light grey), Use for personal (mid grey), Use for work & personal (dark grey), Use for work (black). The horizontal baseline at 0% is a thin dark grey line. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Flat, minimal, no gradients or shadows. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1; the content area is white #FFFFFF. Title is a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern serif such as Didone-style book serif) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height for the cap-height line. Subtitle is the same serif in #545454 at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart labels are a geometric sans (MessinaSans, close to Helvetica-style grotesque) in regular weight, #535353 to #545554, about 1.85% of slide height; chart title is SemiBold at about 2.2%. Footer author name is bold grey #A6A6A6, date and page number are Book weight grey #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. Source line is regular #545454. Accent colour is red #EA4A41 used only for the top series. Gridlines are light grey dotted. Series colours are the five-step grey ramp plus red.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

### Slide 12

**Text**

And a rush to regulate? / The New York Times / The A.I. Race > / How It Began / Key Figures in the Field / One Year of ChatGPT / Regulating / Global Leaders Warn A.I. Could Cause ‘Catastrophic’ Harm / At a U.K. summit, 28 governments, including China and the U.S., signed a declaration agreeing to cooperate on evaluating the risks of artificial intelligence. / Share full article / 178 / nytimes.com / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 12

**Layout**

Two-column split at the slide's vertical midline: a light grey left half holds the title, vertically placed at about 26% from the top and left-aligned at a 4.6% margin; the white right half holds a browser-window screenshot centred in that half, with its top at 4.5% and bottom at about 95.5%. The footer line sits along the bottom right at about 91% from the top, with the author name, date and slide number left to right ending at the right margin near 96.5%.

**Visuals**

One photograph-like screenshot of a web browser window showing a New York Times article, placed as a single rounded rectangle with a soft grey drop shadow. Browser chrome: three small traffic-light dots at top left (red, yellow, green), a rounded address bar with a document icon, a padlock and the text nytimes.com, and a reload icon. Below that, the NYT masthead wordmark with a hamburger menu icon at left and a user/account icon at right, then a section navigation row. Article block: a bold italic serif headline, a serif deck of three lines, a row of pill-shaped buttons (Share full article with a gift icon, a share arrow icon, a bookmark icon, and a comment bubble icon with the count 178). The lower part of the window is a full-width photograph, cropped to a wide band, of a large Victorian-style brick mansion with ornate gables, tall chimneys, bay windows, a green copper-roofed turret at left, and a low arcaded porch at right, under an overcast grey sky. Lighting is flat, diffuse daylight. Dominant colours: grey sky, red-brown brick, green turret, cream stone trim.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2, right half solid white #FFFFFF. Flat two-tone split layout with no gradients. Left panel light grey #F2F2F2; right panel white #FFFFFF. Title text in a light serif, colour dark grey #545454, set large relative to the slide height (about 5.6% of height). Footer text in a sans-serif, mid grey #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of height, with the author name in bold and the date in book weight. No accent colour. The screenshot has rounded window corners and a soft shadow.

### Slide 13

**Text**

Yes, but what is this?
Almost everyone in tech agrees this is The Thing, but it’s much less clear what that means
The next platform shift?
This changes the nature of software?
This takes us to AGI?
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
13

**Layout**

Three equal red circles sit in one horizontal row across the middle of the slide, separated by two grey arrows, and the row spans roughly x 19.5% to 96.2% at y 33.9% to 73.2%. The title and subtitle are left-aligned to a common left edge at x 20.5% near the top, and the footer is right-aligned along the bottom at y 91.1%, ending at x 95.5%; there is no visible slide-number box beyond the footer text and no logo.

**Visuals**

Three filled red circles, each about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, with white text centred inside. Between circle 1 and circle 2, and between circle 2 and circle 3, sits a solid grey right-pointing block arrow (shaft plus triangular head) about 5.2% wide and 9.3% tall, vertically centred on the circles' midline. The arrows point left to right, showing the sequence circle 1 to circle 2 to circle 3. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide. Flat, minimal design on a very light grey background with no gradients, shadows or textures. Title and subtitle are dark grey #545454 in a serif (Messina Modern Regular, a transitional serif); the title is about 5.56% of slide height and the subtitle about 2.59%. The title's word 'is' is set in Messina Modern Italic. Circle labels are white Messina Modern Light, about 3.33% of slide height. Circles are solid red #EB483F with no outline and are perfectly round. Arrows are flat grey #A7A7A7 with no outline. Footer uses Messina Sans Bold for the name and Messina Sans Book for the date and page number, about 1.85% of slide height, in light grey #A6A6A6, with the name in bold and the rest in regular weight.

**Text**

The tech industry moves in platform shifts

Mainframes
PCs
Web
Open Source
Smartphones
Cloud
Generative AI
Benedict Evans –– December 2023
15

**Layout**

A single full-width panel row of six vertical columns sits below a centred title and subtitle block, with the columns spanning from about 20% to 100% of the width and the leftmost 20% of the white band left empty. The title is centred near the top at about 5.6% from the top edge, with a left-aligned subtitle beneath it starting at x 20.6%. The footer line sits right-aligned at about 91% from the top, with the author name, date and slide number in a single line, and the slide number at the far right.

**Visuals**

Five grey curved arrows and one red dashed curved arrow, each rising from left to right as a smooth S-shaped line with a filled triangular arrowhead. Each arrow runs across one column boundary: the first grey arrow runs from the Mainframes column into the PCs column, the second from PCs into Web/Open Source, the third from Web/Open Source into Smartphones/Cloud, the fourth from Smartphones/Cloud into the Generative AI column, and the red dashed arrow rises within the Generative AI column. Grey arrows are solid stroke in mid grey #808080 about 0.4% of slide height thick, with grey arrowheads of about 2.5% slide width. The red arrow is a dotted stroke in #EB483F with a red arrowhead at its upper right end. Each arrow sits above its column label, with the steps rising from lower left to upper right. No photographs, logos or icons. Labels are in text elements listed below, not part of the arrows.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the columns. Light grey background #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height containing the six columns. Adjacent columns are separated by thin #F1F1F1 vertical gaps about 0.4% of width. Title and subtitle are set in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, in #545454 dark grey. The title is about 5.6% of slide height, the subtitle about 2.6%. Column labels are MessinaModern-Book at about 3.0% of slide height in #545454, except Generative AI in #EB483F. Footer is MessinaSans in #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height, with Benedict Evans in bold. Shapes have square corners with no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 16

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A simple S-curve diagram with minimal labels makes a complex idea about technology maturity readable at a glance.
- Review: Charts such as slide 3 are clean and legible, and the S-curve built across slides 16-19 and the strikethrough on slide 30 show the thinking well.
- Worth keeping: Keep the single-idea slides, the red-versus-grey emphasis, the S-curve build on slides 16-19, and the strikethrough reveal on slide 30.

**Text**

New technologies come in S Curves
New tech generally goes from stupid to exciting to boring
Why is this useful?
Exciting!
Boring
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
16

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide divided into a title block across the top and a white main panel in the middle, which is split into three white columns separated by thin grey gutters, with a fourth empty white column at the right edge. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5%, the column labels are centered in columns one to three near y 27%, and a footer band at the bottom holds the presenter name, date and slide number right-aligned to about x 95%.

**Visuals**

One diagram: a single red S-curve (logistic growth curve) with an arrowhead, drawn as a line with no axes, no numbers and no data labels. The curve starts at the lower left of the white panel near the bottom edge, rises gently through the first column, climbs steeply across the boundary between columns two and three, and flattens to horizontal as it reaches an arrowhead pointing right at the right end of column three. Stroke is about 0.4% of slide height, colour red #E8453C, round joins, no shadow. The arrowhead is a solid red filled triangle pointing right. Column labels 'Why is this useful?', 'Exciting!' and 'Boring' sit above the curve, marking the three stages of the S-curve from left to right.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the title and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF main panel. Title and body text colour dark grey #545454; footer text colour light grey #A6A6A6; accent colour red #E8453C used only for the curve and arrowhead. Typefaces: title, subtitle and column labels in Messina Modern (a transitional book serif), regular weight, with the title about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle and column labels about 2.6% to 3.0%. Footer in Messina Sans, with 'Benedict Evans' in bold and the date and number in book weight, about 1.9% of slide height. Line and shape styles: flat, no borders, no shadows, no gradients, no corner radii. Column gutters are thin grey strips of #F1F1F1 about 0.3% of slide width wide.

**Layout**

Three equal vertical columns sit in a white central band spanning roughly 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height, separated by thin light-grey gutters, with the left and right margins left empty white. The title is left-aligned at x 20.5% near the top, with a subtitle beneath it, and a footer line with author, date and slide number sits right-aligned in the lower grey band near the bottom right.

**Visuals**

One diagram: an S-curve line drawn in red starting at the bottom-left of the first column (about x 20%, y 94.5%), rising gently through the first column, steepening through the second column and ending in an arrowhead at about x 76.5%, y 40.5% in the third column. Two circular nodes sit on the curve. A red circle (#EB483F) labelled 'Machine Learning' sits at the boundary of columns two and three, centred about x 55.8%, y 54.5%. A dark grey circle (#535353) labelled 'Smart phones' sits in column three, centred about x 67.3%, y 43.2%, with the curve passing behind its lower-left and emerging from its right side into the arrow. The curve is a smooth red stroke of about 0.3% of slide width, with a solid red triangular arrowhead about 1.4% wide. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 solid, with a white #FFFFFF central band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Footer uses MessinaSans, grey #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height: 'Benedict Evans' in Bold and the rest in Book. Accent colours are red #EB483F for the curve, arrow and machine learning node, and dark grey #535353 for the smartphones node. Shapes are flat circles with no shadows, gradients or outlines. Corner radii: none.

### Slide 19

**Review notes**

- Review: Charts such as slide 3 are clean and legible, and the S-curve built across slides 16-19 and the strikethrough on slide 30 show the thinking well.
- Worth keeping: Keep the single-idea slides, the red-versus-grey emphasis, the S-curve build on slides 16-19, and the strikethrough reveal on slide 30.

**Text**

New technologies come in S Curves
And now we have a new cycle?
Why is this useful?
Exciting!
Boring
Smart
phones
Machine
Learning
Generative
ML
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 19

**Layout**

A full-width white band sits between a light grey header and a light grey footer. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left, above three column headings, each centred over one of the first three vertical columns, with a fourth empty column on the right. Four thin light-grey column dividers separate the columns. The footer credit and page number are right-aligned near the bottom right, with no logo and no slide-number box.

**Visuals**

One diagram: an S-curve drawn as a red line rising from the bottom left to the upper right, ending in an arrowhead pointing right. Three dark grey circles and one red circle sit on the curve as labelled nodes, in order from left to right: red circle 'Generative ML' (lowest, on the curve at the boundary of columns one and two), dark grey circle 'Machine Learning' (middle, straddling columns two and three), and dark grey circle 'Smart phones' (highest, in column three). The curve passes behind and through each circle, and the arrowhead ends just short of the right column divider. No axes, legend or values.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer areas, with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle of the slide. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands framing a white (#FFFFFF) central band. Titles and column headings in #545454 (serif). Node labels in white sans-serif. Accent red #EB483F for the curve, arrowhead and the Generative ML node; nodes Machine Learning and Smart phones are dark grey #535353. Footer text in light grey #A6A6A6. Title is a Messina Modern serif, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%; column headings about 3.0%; node labels about 2.2% in Messina Sans regular; footer about 1.9% with 'Benedict Evans' in bold. Lines are flat with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 20

**Text**

2013: why is Machine
Learning useful?
“That’s clever, but so what?”
Source: Imagenet
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 20

**Layout**

Two-column split: the left half (0 to 50% width) is a light grey panel holding the title, quote and source line; the right half is white and holds a single framed image. The title sits in the upper-middle of the left column at about 25.6% from the top, left-aligned at a 4.6% left margin. The footer runs along the bottom with the author name, date and page number right-aligned at about 91% from the top; the source line sits bottom-left at the same baseline band.

**Visuals**

One photograph with bounding boxes overlaid (object-detection example). Subject: an indoor scene of a wooden-plank floor with a sleeping tan/brown dog lying on its side at lower left, a small child in an orange top sitting at centre, a dark office chair at upper right, and a dark chair leg at upper left, seen through a window with white light at the top. Composition: wide landscape frame, full scene visible, with a black vertical strip on the right side (about 18% of the image width) containing the labels. Lighting: even daylight from the window, warm brown floor tones. Dominant colours: brown wood, orange, white window, black strip. The image is boxed (not full-bleed) with a soft grey drop shadow beneath and around it. Overlays: four coloured rectangles with no fill, stroke about 0.4% of slide height: green box around the upper-left chair leg (about x 4.6 to 11.3, y 26 to 44); green box around the upper-right chair (about x 56.3 to 75.3, y 26 to 50); yellow box around the child (about x 43.5 to 61.8, y 37 to 59); red box around the dog (about x 56.7 to 73.0, y 50 to 74). Labels in the black strip next to the image: 'person' in yellow at the top, 'dog' in red in the middle, 'chair' in green at the bottom, each in a sans-serif face. The boxes overlap the image content and each other (the yellow box overlaps the green chair box and the red dog box touches the yellow box edge).

**Design**

Background of the left panel is light grey #F2F2F2 and of the right panel white #FFFFFF. Title text is dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif face, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height per line (line pitch about 6.6%). Quote is dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, about 3.0% of slide height. Source line is dark grey #545454 in MessinaSans-Regular, about 1.85% of slide height. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6; date in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6 preceded by an en-dash pair; page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Image has a soft drop shadow. No gradients, no rounded corners.

### Slide 21

**Text**

2013: why is Machine Learning useful? / What’s the right level of abstraction to understand this? / Image / recognition / works now! / And so do / lots of other / unsolved / problems / This is / pattern / recognition / What can we / turn into / pattern / recognition? / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 / 21

**Layout**

Four equal circles sit in one horizontal row across the middle-lower part of the slide, joined by three arrows, spanning roughly x 19.8% to 96% with generous margins. The title sits top-left at x 20.5%, y 5.6%, with a smaller subtitle beneath it left-aligned to the same edge. The footer runs along the bottom right at y 91%: bold author name, date with a dash separator, and slide number at the far right edge.

**Visuals**

Diagram: a left-to-right process of four circles connected by three grey block arrows. Circle 1 (dark grey #535353) reads 'Image recognition works now!'. Circle 2 (dark grey #535353) reads 'And so do lots of other unsolved problems'. Circle 3 (dark grey #535353) reads 'This is pattern recognition'. Circle 4 (red #EB483F) reads 'What can we turn into pattern recognition?'. Each arrow is a solid right-pointing block arrow in light grey #A6A6A6, sitting in the gap between adjacent circles, with its point aimed at the next circle. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal, light grey background with no gradients or shadows. Title and subtitle in grey #545454 serif (Messina Modern Regular style). Circle text in white #FFFFFF serif (Messina Modern Book). Dark circles #535353 with the final circle accented in red #EB483F. Arrows #A6A6A6. Footer in light grey #A6A6A6 sans-serif (Messina Sans, bold for the name, book for date and number). Title is about 5.6% of slide height; body and circle text about 3.0%; footer about 1.9%. Circles are perfect ellipses with no outlines.

### Slide 27

**Text**

This paper does not exist / What is an answer likely to look like? Well… / Economics history papers have the most citations / Douglass North is one of the most highly cited authors / Douglass North’s most-cited co-authored paper was published in The Journal of Economic History / Economics papers most commonly have two co-authors… / So what’s the pattern? / Source: David Smerdon / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 / 27 / SM / What is the most cited economics paper of all time / The most cited economics paper of all time is "A Theory of Economic History" by Douglass North and Robert Thomas, which was published in The Journal of Economic History in 1969. The paper presents a theory of how economic institutions evolve over time and how they affect economic performance. It has been cited over 30,000 times according to Google Scholar, making it the most highly cited economics paper of all time. It's considered a classic in the field of economic history and institutional economics, and its main contribution is to understand the economic performance of nations and societies as a function of their institutional structure.

### Slide 30

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Building a question across three slides with progressive reveal creates a clear rhetorical rhythm that a presenter can reuse.
- Review: Charts such as slide 3 are clean and legible, and the S-curve built across slides 16-19 and the strikethrough on slide 30 show the thinking well.
- Worth keeping: Keep the single-idea slides, the red-versus-grey emphasis, the S-curve build on slides 16-19, and the strikethrough reveal on slide 30.

**Text**

Answer this question / What sort of text in the training data / seems likely to be related to questions that / look like this? / What would an answer to this look like? / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 30

**Layout**

A single white panel sits on a light grey field, inset from the left and right edges, with its left edge at 17.4% and its right edge at 95.6% of slide width. Four lines of left-aligned serif text start at x 20.5% inside the panel, with the first line at y 30.4% and the closing question at y 70.4%. The footer runs along the bottom right at y 91.1%, right-aligned to the panel's right edge, with the page number 30 at the far right; there is no separate title or logo.

**Visuals**

none. The only graphic marks are the strikethrough rules drawn through the first three text blocks ("Answer this question" and the three-line question), which are part of the text formatting.

**Design**

Background #F2F2F2 light grey with a flat #FFFFFF white panel and no gradients, shadows or corner radii. All text is in #EB483F red-coral. Main text is MessinaModern-Book, a modern serif, at about 5.56% of slide height, with the closing phrase 'look like?' in MessinaModern-BookItalic. The footer is MessinaSans in #A6A6A6 grey at about 1.85% of slide height, with 'Benedict Evans' in MessinaSans-Bold and the date and page number in MessinaSans-Book. Line pitch within the question is about 6.7% of slide height. The three struck-through blocks use red strikethrough rules at the same colour as the text.

**Layout**

Full-slide light grey (#F1F1F1) field with a white chart band between about 20.5% and 86.1% of the height. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5% in the top grey band, with the title above the subtitle. The chart fills the white band from x 20.5% to about 95% with its legend at the far left (x 4.8%); the chart title sits above the plot at x 20.6%. The footer sits in the bottom grey band: source line at left (x 20.5%) and author, date and page number right-aligned to about 95.5%. The slide number is 31.

**Visuals**

Clustered vertical bar chart. Title: “Generative AI always produces factually accurate answers” (UK, June 2023). Y axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 50%, gridlines every 10% (dotted light grey lines). X axis: three categories, Agree, Neither or don't know, Disagree; no axis unit label printed. Series 1 'Aware of' (dark grey #535353): Agree about 28%, Neither or don't know about 35%, Disagree about 37%. Series 2 'Have used' (red #EB483F): Agree about 43%, Neither or don't know about 25%, Disagree about 32%. No data values are printed on the bars; these are estimates read from bar heights against the gridlines. Legend at upper left of chart: dark grey square labelled Aware of, red square labelled Have used, stacked vertically. No highlighted points. Bars are flat with no outline, with a thin white gap between paired bars. The horizontal 0% baseline is a thin grey line.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands; chart band white #FFFFFF. Title in MessinaModern Regular serif, colour #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). ### Slide 33

**Text**

Machine learning, automated learning / Instead of telling a computer how to think, give it the data and get it to work it out for itself / 2013: ML 1.0 / How do we tell a computer how to recognise a cat? / Give the computer a million pictures of cats / 2023: ML 2.0 / How do we tell a computer how to reason and understand? / Give the computer the entire written output of all human intelligence (!) / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 33

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands. A light grey header band across the top holds a left-aligned title and a one-line subtitle, both starting at x 20.5%. Below it a white panel spans the full width from y 20.5% to 86.1% and is split by a thin grey gap at y 52.2% into two rows, each with a label column at x 4.8%, a question column at x 20.5%, a red dotted arrow at x 49.4% to 65%, and an answer column at x 73.3%. The footer text sits right-aligned at the bottom right, around y 91%, with a margin of about 5% from the right edge.

**Visuals**

Two identical diagram rows, each a left-to-right flow: a question in serif text, then a red dotted arrow, then an answer in serif text. Arrow 1 (row 2013): dotted line in red #E8453C, about 0.5% of slide height thick, from x 49.4% to 65.0% at y about 36.1%, ending in a small solid filled red triangular arrowhead pointing right. Arrow 2 (row 2023): same dotted red line and arrowhead from x 49.4% to 65.0% at y about 70.2%. No photographs, charts, icons or logos.

**Design**

Background is a flat light grey #F1F1F1 with white #FFFFFF middle panel and a thin #F2F2F2 separator stripe. Title and subtitle text #545454 in a serif typeface recognisable as Messina Modern (Regular), a transitional-style serif with a regular weight. Body and labels #545454 in Messina Modern Book, a light serif. Accent colour is red #E8453C for the dotted arrows. Footer uses Messina Sans, with 'Benedict Evans' in Bold and the date and page number in Book, all in grey #A6A6A6. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and body text about 2.6% to 3.3% of slide height, footer about 1.9%. Shapes are flat, with square corners, no shadows and no gradients.

### Slide 34

**Text**

2023: how should we understand generative ML?
What’s the right level of abstraction to understand this?
Pattern creation?
Synthesis &
summary?
Reasoning?
Something else?
What can we do
with this?
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
34

**Layout**

Single slide with a left-aligned serif title and subtitle across the top, starting at about x 20.5%. Three circles sit in one horizontal row across the vertical centre, linked by two arrows, with the first circle at about x 19.5% and the last ending near x 96%. The footer sits bottom right at about y 91%: author name in bold, date, and page number 34 at the far right edge.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles connected left to right by two grey block arrows. Circle 1 (dark grey #535353) reads 'Pattern creation?'. Circle 2 (dark grey #535353) reads 'Synthesis & summary?' / 'Reasoning?' / 'Something else?' stacked. Circle 3 (red #EB483F) reads 'What can we do with this?' The two arrows are right-pointing block arrows in light grey #A7A7A7, each with a rectangular shaft and triangular head, positioned in the gaps between circles. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1, flat with no gradient. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Circle text in MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.33% of slide height. Footer in MessinaSans sans-serif, #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height; author name bold, rest book weight. Circles are flat solid fills with no outline or shadow. Arrows are flat solid fills with no outline. No corner radii, gradients or shadows.

### Slide 36

**Text**

2023 use cases
Step one: duct-tape the new tool to the problems you already understand. Step two:…
Early wins
Code assistants
Brainstorming
Autosuggest
‘Thin wrappers’
Add some UI and
data linkage to
OpenAI APIs
Synthesis
Point an LLM at
your corpus and
get answers
2024?
What new
problems can we
discover?
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
36

**Layout**

A single-row composition of four equal circles centred horizontally across the lower-middle of the slide, with the title and subtitle left-aligned to a common left edge at about 20.5% of slide width in the upper area. The footer sits bottom-right on one line at about 91% of slide height, with the page number at the far right edge (about 94.3% to 95.5% of width), giving a right margin of roughly 4.5%.

**Visuals**

Four white circles in a horizontal row, each with a thin dark grey outline (about 2 px, #545454). The first, second and third circles have dark grey outlines; the fourth has a red outline (#E74C3C) of the same weight. Each circle holds a bold heading and one to three lines of centred text. The circles are not photographs, charts or icons; they are plain geometric shapes with no fill gradient, shadow or overlay. Circle 1 (Early wins) and circle 3 (Synthesis) and circle 4 (2024?) sit at the same vertical level; circle 2 sits slightly higher. No logos, icons or images appear.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 across the entire slide. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1). Titles and headings in dark grey #545454; body text #545454; footer name in light grey #A6A6A6; the single accent is the red outline (#E74C3C) on the fourth circle. Typeface for titles, headings and body is a transitional serif, closely matching MessinaModern (Regular and SemiBold), approximately Century-style serif. Footer is a neo-grotesque sans-serif, MessinaSans Bold for the name and Book for date and number. Title about 5.56% of slide height (cap height roughly 3.9%); subtitle 2.59%; circle headings 3.33% semibold; circle body 2.96% regular; footer 1.85%. Circles have no corner radius (true circles), no shadow, no gradient.

The footer row at about 91% of height holds 'Source: LinkedIn' at the left margin and 'Benedict Evans –– December 2023' followed by the slide number 37 at the right, ending near 95.6% of width.

One photograph-style UI screenshot of a LinkedIn 'Post' composer dialog, boxed (not full-bleed) with a soft grey drop shadow, centred at about x 18.7 to 69.2 percent and y 18.3 to 89 percent. Content: a white modal card with a circular headshot of Benedict Evans (a man with glasses and short grey hair, cropped to a circle, about 3.5% of slide width) beside the name 'Benedict Evans' with a dropdown triangle and the line 'Post to Anyone'; a close X at top right; the prompt 'Start typing or draft with AI' with a magic-wand sparkle icon, 'draft with AI' underlined; a pale blue rounded callout reading 'Let AI help you with a first draft' with a small X and two lines of body text; an empty writing area; a smiley emoji icon at lower left; a row of four pale beige circular buttons holding an image icon, a video camera icon, a calendar icon and an ellipsis; a divider line; a clock icon and a greyed 'Post' pill button at lower right. Lighting is flat with no visible light source; dominant colours are white, near-black text, grey icons and a pale blue callout. The screenshot is not cropped. The measurements file lists two placed copies of the image (970 by 762 px and 964 by 765 px) at almost the same position; the second is slightly offset, which suggests a duplicate shadow layer.

No accent colours beyond these greys and the pale blue.

“Every single team at Amazon is exploring generative AI” - Amazon CEO

38

**Layout**

Two-part layout on a light grey field with a left-aligned title and subtitle at the top, a wide boxed image centred beneath them, and a footer row with the source at left and author, date and page number at right. The title starts at about x 20.5% and the image spans roughly x 19% to 86%, with margins of about 14% on the right. The footer sits at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

One photograph, a split-image composite in a wide landscape box with a soft drop shadow. The left portion shows a stainless-steel four-slot toaster with the brand GRUX in embossed lettering, shot as a cut-out product photo on a white-to-light-grey gradient, with black feet and dark side knobs, two toast-level dials and a small label panel. A thick diagonal yellow stripe, running from the top edge near x 61% down to the bottom edge near x 40% of the photo, divides the image. The right portion shows the same toaster on a dark wooden table in an autumn setting: a grey linen napkin, toast topped with cream cheese and orange slices of pumpkin or squash, a grey metal spoon, a fork at lower right, a dried orange-brown leaf at upper left, a large orange pumpkin cropped at the right edge, and a weathered wooden tray on a grey wall at upper right. Lighting is soft and warm in the lifestyle half and even and bright in the cut-out half. Dominant colours are silver, grey, warm brown, orange and bright yellow. The photo is boxed, not full-bleed, and is cropped at the edges of its rectangle.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Plain flat background in light grey #F1F1F1 with no gradient or pattern. Title and subtitle are in a book-style high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a Modern serif), dark grey #545454, regular weight, not italic. The title is about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle about 2.6%. Footer text is sans-serif (MessinaSans) at about 1.85% of slide height: the source line is regular in #545454, the author name is bold and the date and page number are book weight, all in light grey #A6A6A6. Accent colour is yellow #F5C232 used for the diagonal stripe. Image box has square corners and a soft grey drop shadow around its edges. No other shapes, lines or gradients.

**Text**

Incumbents make it a feature
Startups use it to unbundle
Sometimes, you change the nature of the market
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 39

Three equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle-upper band of the slide, evenly spaced with the left circle starting near 19.5% and the right circle ending near 96.2% of the width. Three filled circles, no photographs or charts. Left circle: dark grey #535353, diameter about 22.1% of slide width, containing white serif text 'Incumbents make it a feature'. Right circle: red-coral #EB483F, same size, containing white serif text 'Sometimes, you change the nature of the market'. Overall minimalist, high contrast between dark grey, red and off-white background.

### Slide 41

**Text**

And Adobe is trying to do both / Adding Generative AI features to Photoshop while launching Firefly (and buying Figma) / Make it a feature / Generative Fill etc / in Photoshop / Unbundle / New dedicated app / in ‘Firefly’ / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 41 / Jaguar drinking from pond / Generate

**Layout**

Header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5 percent of slide height) in light grey with the title and subtitle left-aligned at about x 20.5 percent. A white content band fills the middle (about 20.5 to 86 percent). Three elements sit on one horizontal row: a circle at left, a photo with UI overlay in the centre, and a circle at right, with grey block arrows between them pointing outward from the photo toward each circle. Footer band in light grey at the bottom (about 86 to 100 percent) holds the author, date and page number right-aligned near the right margin of about 5 percent.

**Visuals**

Three shape groups and one photograph. (1) Left circle: an outlined circle, white fill, dark grey stroke about 3 px, containing the text Make it a feature and Generative Fill etc in Photoshop. (2) Left block arrow: solid grey #A7A7A7 left-pointing block arrow, about 5.2 percent wide by 9.3 percent high, flat shaft and triangular head. (3) Centre photograph: a 600 by 452 pixel rendered image, unboxed with square corners and no border, of a spotted jaguar standing and drinking from a dark green pond in a jungle setting with tall wooden bookshelves full of books on the left and right, green leaves and a small butterfly at the right edge; a white dotted ellipse (selection marquee) is drawn around the jaguar and pond; the image is lit evenly with a soft green-gold tone and dominant colours dark green, brown and gold. The image is not cropped. Overlaid across its lower part is a dark grey (#3A3A3A approx) rounded-corner generative panel with a dark prompt field reading Jaguar drinking from pond, a grey Generate button with a star-sparkle icon and the word Generate in white, and a row of three thumbnail images of the jaguar; the middle thumbnail has a blue (#1E88E5 approx) selection border. The panel has a soft drop shadow. (4) Right block arrow: solid grey #A7A7A7 right-pointing block arrow, same size as left arrow. (5) Right circle: outlined circle, white fill, red (#E84C3D approx) stroke about 3 px, containing Unbundle and New dedicated app in ‘Firefly’.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with white #FFFFFF central band. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white central band. Titles in dark grey #545454, body text in dark grey #545454, footer text in light grey #A6A6A6. Typefaces: title and body in MessinaModern, a serif with moderate contrast, title regular weight at about 5.6 percent of slide height, subtitle regular at about 2.6 percent, circle headings SemiBold at about 3.3 percent, circle body regular at about 3.0 percent. Footer in MessinaSans sans-serif, Bold for the author name and Book for the date and page number, about 1.85 percent. Accents: grey arrows #A7A7A7, red circle stroke #E84C3D, blue selection border in the UI thumbnail. Shapes have no corner radius for the circles (perfect circles), arrows have square block edges, no gradients, no shadows on the circles or arrows.

### Slide 42

**Text**

But then, new tools change the nature of the work / First we make the new tool fit the work - then we change the work to fit the tool / Source: The Apartment, 1960 / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 / 42

**Layout**

Full-width slide with a light grey background. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5% with a generous top margin, leaving the left edge above the photo empty. A full-bleed black-and-white photograph spans the full slide width between y 17% and y 93%. The source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5%, and the footer (author, date, page number) sits bottom-right, ending near x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

One black-and-white photograph, full width (0 to 100% of slide width), from y 17.0% to y 93.1%, unboxed and uncropped at the left and right edges. It shows a large open-plan office from The Apartment (1960): rows of desks with typewriters and telephones receding toward a vanishing point, many clerks in dark suits and dresses working at them, and a grid of fluorescent ceiling light panels supported by square columns. Composition is a one-point perspective centred on the middle of the frame, with the foreground desks at the bottom. Lighting is flat, even fluorescent light, rendered in greyscale with mid to high contrast. Dominant colours are grey, black and white. No overlay or tint. No logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a flat light grey (#F1F1F1). Title and footer text are dark grey (#545454) and light grey (#A6A6A6) respectively. Title is set in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif-like modern face, at about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight, not capitalised. Subtitle is MessinaModern-Regular at about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Source and footer lines use MessinaSans (Regular, Bold for the author name, Book for the date and page number) at about 1.9% of slide height. No shapes, lines, corner radii, shadows or gradients; the photograph is a plain rectangle.

### Slide 43

**Text**

Platform shifts tend to change everything else
New kinds of building blocks can go a lot further than people expect
“SQL makes arbitrary data queries easier”
Just-in-time supply chains…
“We put multitouch and macOS on a phone”
Uber, Instagram, Tiktok… and 5bn people online
“We made a thing that can predict your next word”
???
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 43

**Layout**

Full-width three-row layout with a title block on a light grey top band. The title and subtitle sit left-aligned at x 20.5%, top at y 5.6%. Below, three equal horizontal rows sit on a white band from y 20.5% to 86.1%, separated by thin grey gaps at y 40.7% and 63.4%. Each row has a left quote at x 20.5%, a red dotted arrow in the centre (x 49.4% to 65.0%), and a right-hand result at x 73.3%. The left quotes are left-aligned at x 20.5%, and the left margin above the content is empty. The footer, bottom-right at y 91.1%, reads author name, date and slide number on one line, right-aligned to about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Three identical horizontal dotted arrows, one per row, in red #EB483F. Each is a dotted line about 15.6% of slide width running from x 49.4% to x 65.0%, ending in a small solid right-pointing triangular arrowhead. Arrow 1 is at y about 30.2%, arrow 2 at y about 53.1%, arrow 3 at y about 75.8%. They point from the left quote to the right result in each row. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 with a full-width white band #FFFFFF behind the three rows and thin #F2F2F2 separator bars. Title in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, weight regular, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Quote and result body text in MessinaModern-Book, a serif book weight, #545454, about 3.3% of slide height. The '???' is #EB483F. 45

**Layout**

Single content slide with a left-aligned title and subtitle set at about 20.5% from the left edge, leaving the left fifth of the slide empty. A horizontal row of four equal circles sits across the middle of the slide, spanning roughly x 19.8% to 96% with equal gaps of about 4% between them, joined by three block arrows. The footer (author, date and page number) sits bottom-right at about y 91%, right-aligned to about 95.6% of the width, with no logo or footer rule.

**Visuals**

Four solid circles connected left to right by three grey right-pointing block arrows (a flat rectangular shaft with a triangular head). Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 and labelled Web. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 and labelled Google & / Facebook. Circle 3 is dark grey #535353 and labelled YouTube / Instagram / TikTok. Circle 4 is red #EB483F, the only coloured element, and labelled LLMs. Each circle is about 324 px in diameter on a 2000 by 1125 px slide, and all four share the same vertical centre near y 53%. The three arrows are light grey #A6A6A6, each about 4% of slide width by 7% of slide height, centred vertically on the circles, located between circles 1 and 2, 2 and 3, and 3 and 4. The flow is a left-to-right diagram: Web to Google & Facebook to YouTube / Instagram / TikTok to LLMs.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or textures. Background is light grey #F1F1F1. Title is set in a serif (MessinaModern Regular, a transitional-style serif) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle uses the same serif in #545454 at about 2.6% of slide height. Circle labels are MessinaModern Book in white #FFFFFF at about 3% of slide height, centred. Circles are #535353 with the last one #EB483F; arrows are #A6A6A6. Footer uses MessinaSans, a sans-serif: Benedict Evans in Bold and the date and page number in Book, all #A6A6A6 at about 1.9% of slide height. Shapes have no outlines, corner radii or rotation (circles are true circles, arrows are block arrows).

### Slide 46

**Text**

**Layout**

Two equal columns: a light grey left half (0 to 50% width) carrying the title, subtitle and source line, and a white right half (50 to 100%) holding a centred phone image. **Visuals**

One photograph of a silver and black BlackBerry Curve smartphone with a Verizon badge, shown front-on and fully upright, centred in the right half. The screen shows a status bar with signal bars and 1XEV at top left and a battery icon at top right, a light panel reading 12:21 PM FRI MAY 25 with a '3' envelope-style notification icon and a Bluetooth symbol, a faint red Verizon check-mark watermark, the words Messages (3) in bold, and a row of five app icons (phone, envelope with red bow, phone with arrows, red telephone, calendar showing 25). The keyboard is a full QWERTY layout with dark grey keys, letters and symbols on each key, a SPACE bar, SYM and ALT keys, a central navigation trackball and red and green call keys above. The 'verizon' wordmark in red and black appears on the chin below the keyboard. Lighting is flat and even studio lighting with soft shadows, the phone is dominated by silver metallic greys and black, on a plain white background. The photo is a full, uncropped cut-out with no overlay or tint, placed directly on the white panel.

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2 and right half solid white #FFFFFF. Left panel background light grey #F2F2F2, right panel plain white #FFFFFF with no gradients or shadows. Title and subtitle text colour dark grey #545454. Footer and page number text light grey #A6A6A6. Source line dark grey #545454. Title in Messina Modern Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight, large, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in Messina Modern Book, a serif, book weight, about 3% of slide height. Footer in Messina Sans, with Bold for the author name and Book for date and page number, about 1.85% of slide height. No accent colours, rules or shapes on the slide.

### Slide 47

**Text**

Posit: all AI questions have one of two answers / Most AI questions match to a pattern - unless you want to know if the pattern will be broken / Answer: / “It will work / like every other / platform shift” / Insert your / question here… / Answer: / “No-one / knows” / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 47

**Layout**

A single horizontal row of three equal circles centred vertically in the middle band of the slide, with two grey arrows between them. The title sits top-left at about 20.5% from the left edge, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned to the same edge. The footer runs along the bottom right, with the author name, date and page number on one baseline. The left margin is about 20% and the right margin about 4%. There is no logo and no separate footer rule.

**Visuals**

Diagram: three filled circles in a row, left to right, with two block arrows between them. Left circle is dark grey #535353 and holds the text 'Answer: / “It will work like every other platform shift”'. Centre circle is red #EB483F and holds 'Insert your question here…'. Right circle is dark grey #535353 and holds 'Answer: / “No-one knows”'. Between the left circle and the centre circle is a left-pointing block arrow in #A7A7A7 whose head points at the left circle. Between the centre circle and the right circle is a right-pointing block arrow in #A7A7A7 whose head points at the right circle. The arrows point outward from the centre, so the centre circle is the question and each side is one of its two answers. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Flat solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look. Background is a flat light grey with no gradient, texture or pattern. Title is set in a serif (Messina Modern Regular) in mid-dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, with no capitals and no letter-spacing. ### Slide 51

**Text**

Return of the ‘agent’ (or the / mainframe)?
The 30-year dream of the general purpose ‘AI Agent’ / that can do anything for you
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 51

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: a left grey panel (about 50% width) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 4.6% from the left edge, with the title in the upper-middle (starting at about 25.6% from the top) and the subtitle below it. The right half is white and holds a single cartoon image centred in that area. The footer (author, date, slide number) sits along the bottom right at about 91% from the top, with the slide number at the right margin.

**Visuals**

One illustration: a cartoon paperclip character with googly eyes and raised eyebrows, standing on a sheet of yellow-green lined notepaper that curves upward to the right, casting a soft grey shadow to the lower right. Beside its head is a pale yellow speech bubble with a dark thin outline and rounded corners reading 'Would you like me to manage your kid's high school application?' with two boxed buttons, 'Yes' on the left and 'No' on the right, with a pointer from the bubble to the paperclip's eye. The paperclip is lavender-grey with dark outline; the paper is pale yellow-green with thin olive lines and a faint red margin line. Lighting is flat and even, composition is a boxed image on white with no cropping, no overlay, not full-bleed. Dominant colours: lavender grey, pale yellow, olive green, white.

**Design**

Background split: left panel light grey #F2F2F2 covering half the slide width, right half white #FFFFFF. Title colour dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular serif, about 5.56% of slide height per line (large). Subtitle body text #545454 in MessinaModern-Book serif, about 2.96% of slide height. Footer text in MessinaSans, grey #A6A6A6: author name bold, then date and slide number in book weight, about 1.85% of slide height. No accent colours, no lines, no shapes, no shadows, no gradients; the image is an unboxed illustration with square corners.

The body is white and contains the black OpenAI knot logo inside a thin-outlined circle, the heading 'How can I help you today?' in bold black sans-serif, and a 2x2 grid of thin-bordered rounded suggestion cards, each with a bold title and a grey subtitle. Subtitle in Messina Modern Book serif, #545454, about 3.0% of slide height, set on two lines with tight leading. Footer in Messina Sans, name in Bold and the date and page number in Book, colour #A6A6A6 light grey, about 1.9% of slide height. Screenshot uses its own neutral palette (white, light grey #F0F0F0 chrome, black text, grey #8E8E93 secondary text) with rounded corners of about 1% of slide width and a soft large drop shadow. No accent colour.

The name box reads A1 and the active cell A1 is outlined in green. The grid shows empty columns A to Q and rows 1 to 38 with light grey gridlines on white. Sheet tab reads Sheet1 in green; status bar reads Ready and 100%. Lighting is flat and even; dominant colours are white, light grey and Excel green. The screenshot is boxed, not cropped and not full-bleed, and has no overlay or tint.

Window chrome: red, yellow and green traffic-light dots at top-left; title Microsoft Excel centred at top; search box at top-right. Left sidebar (light grey #E8E8E8 band) with circular avatar BE and items Benedict, Home, New (selected, grey highlight with green plus icon and green label), Recent (clock icon), Shared (people icon), Open (folder icon). Main area is a 4-column grid of template thumbnails, each with a caption beneath: Row 1: Blank Workbook (blank grid of columns A to C and rows 1 to 7, outlined in a green #107C41 border with green label pill), Welcome to Excel (white card with a green New corner ribbon, large text Take a tour and a green circular arrow icon), Make a List (white card, blue heading), Track My Tasks (white card with yellow highlighted cells). Row 2: Manage My Money (white card, black and green pie chart, 55% and Total Monthly Income figures), Personal Budget (dark grey card with blue ring chart at 62%), Family Budget (white card with teal and green bar chart, Cash Flow and Monthly Income headings), Credit Card Payoff Calculator (white card with orange bar chart). Row 3: Mortgage Loan Calculator (white card with green header and table), College Course Manager (dark card with teal header FALL TERM), College Comparison Tracker (white card with green header Applications), Household Organiser (white card with blue BUDGET heading and table). Row 4 is cut off by the bottom toolbar and shows four partly visible unlabeled thumbnails: a navy-headed weekly assignment schedule, a red-headed Daily Schedule, a January calendar, and a pink-headed Time Sheet. Bottom toolbar (white strip) has Cancel button (white with grey border) and Create button (solid green #107C41 with white text). Colours: predominantly white and light grey, with green accents and small coloured chart fills. Lighting is flat, even screen capture.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F2F2F2 full-slide fill with a white #FFFFFF panel covering the left third. Two-tone flat layout. Background is light grey #F2F2F2 across the slide, with a pure white #FFFFFF panel covering the left third. Title and body text are dark grey #545454. Footer text is light grey #A6A6A6. Title is a high-contrast modern serif (MessinaModern Regular) at about 5.6% of slide height; body is the same serif family in Book weight at about 3.0% of slide height. Footer is a sans-serif (MessinaSans) at about 1.9%, bold for the name and book weight for the rest. No gradients, no decorative shapes; the screenshot window has rounded corners and a soft shadow.

### Slide 59

**Text**

Bundling or unbundling? / Do LLMs mean all these apps are swallowed by a few big models - or do they enable an explosion of new apps? / Average number of SaaS apps used per company / 2021 / 2023 / 500 / 400 / 300 / 200 / 100 / 0 / SME / Mid-market / Large enterprises / Source: Productiv / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 59

**Layout**

A full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) header band holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about x 20.5% so both start on one left edge. A white band fills the middle from y 20.5% to 86.1%, containing a chart whose title sits at left x 20.6%, with the legend at far left (x 4.8%) and the plot area running from about x 20.5% to 95%. A grey footer band below holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number right-aligned to about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Chart: clustered vertical bar chart. Title: 'Average number of SaaS apps used per company'. Y-axis: numeric scale 0 to 500 in steps of 100, labelled 0, 100, 200, 300, 400, 500, no unit printed (the values are counts of apps per company). Dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each 100 step. X-axis: three categories, SME, Mid-market, Large enterprises, with a solid grey baseline at 0. Series: 2021 in dark grey #535353 and 2023 in red #EB483F, shown as adjacent bars in each category. Printed values: none; estimates: SME 2021 about 242, SME 2023 about 252; Mid-market 2021 about 238, Mid-market 2023 about 334; Large enterprises 2021 about 315, Large enterprises 2023 about 473. Legend: two square swatches at far left with labels 2021 (dark grey) and 2023 (red), stacked vertically. Highlighted points: none; the 2023 series is the only coloured series and is emphasised by its red colour. Bars are flat with no outlines, and gaps separate the two bars within each group.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF content band between them. Title text is dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif (Modern-style transitional/didone-influenced serif), regular weight, large at about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels are in MessinaSans sans-serif at about 1.9 to 2.2% of slide height, in #545554 or #535353; the chart title is SemiBold. Footer text is MessinaSans, Regular or Bold, in light grey #A6A6A6; the author name is bold. Accent colour is red #EB483F, used only for the 2023 series and its legend swatch. Shapes are rectangles with square corners, no shadows, no gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

### Slide 60

_Long standard legal text on this slide is summarised, not transcribed; its exact words are in the measured list below._

**Text**

A product paradox, perhaps?
Does ‘general-purpose’ AI mean general-purpose tools, or must it be wrapped in single-purpose product?
Platform shift?
Actual product needs
lots of single-purpose
tooling, UX and GTM,
just as before
Truly general
purpose
software that
can do
‘anything’
Paradigm shift?
Far more tasks and
jobs can be
automated by far
fewer apps
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
60

**Layout**

Single row of three circles centred vertically in the lower-middle area, with the title block top-left at about 20.5% from the left edge and a subtitle beneath it; all text is left-aligned at the title margin except the centred circle text. Large empty left margin (about 20%) and right margin of about 6%; footer sits bottom-right at about 91% height with the author name, date and slide number 60 in one line, with no logo. No legal text is present.

**Visuals**

Three circles and two arrows. Left: white-filled circle with a dark grey (#545454) outline about 2 px wide, containing the heading 'Platform shift?' and body text. Centre: circle with no fill and a dark grey (#545454) dotted outline about 2 px wide, containing bold text 'Truly general purpose software that can do ‘anything’'. Right: white-filled circle with a red (#E84C3D) outline about 2 px wide, containing 'Paradigm shift?' and body text. Between the left and centre circles is a solid grey (#A7A7A7) block arrow pointing left; between the centre and right circles is a solid grey (#A7A7A7) block arrow pointing right. Arrows are flat with no shadow. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat fill #F1F1F1 light grey. Flat light grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454, serif typeface resembling Messina Modern (a Scotch-style serif, regular weight for title and subtitle, book weight for body, semibold for headings). Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle headings about 3.3%, circle body about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. Footer in light grey #A6A6A6 sans-serif (Messina Sans), bold for the author name and book weight for the rest. Circles have no shadow; the arrows are solid grey #A7A7A7 with no outlines.
