### Slide 61

**Text**

Unless, of course…
Source: Benedict Evans / Midjourney
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 61

**Layout**

Two-column split: left panel (0–50% width) light grey with title placed at upper-middle left and source line bottom-left; right half white holding a full-height-ish isometric illustration. Title left-aligned at 4.6% left margin, roughly 25.6% from top. Source line sits at bottom-left margin around 91% from top. Footer text (author, date, page number) runs along the bottom right of the white half, right-aligned to the page number at about 95.5% x.

**Visuals**

One illustration: a teal/cyan humanoid alien with a ridged, segmented body and long clawed fingers, emerging from the open door of a white-grey server rack cabinet. Interior of the cabinet shows grey rack units with small displays (some with teal, yellow and pink indicator lights) and a dark starry background behind the creature. The cabinet is shown in isometric three-quarter view, with the right door swung open and the left side panel visible with louvres and vents. The creature's head with a single magenta eye is at upper centre, its arms reaching down over the floor grille at the lower front. Lighting is soft and even, flat studio-style with no strong cast shadow; dominant colours are teal #7FCFCB, light grey #D9D9D9, white #FFFFFF and dark navy #1E2A38 in the starfield. The image is boxed, not cropped, sitting on the white right half with its bottom near 94.5% of slide height, with no overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background light grey #F2F2F2 on left half and white #FFFFFF on right half. Title in a serif, Messina Modern Regular, dark grey #545454, around 5.6% of slide height, lowercase with ellipsis. Body and source text in Messina Sans Regular, #545454, about 1.85% of slide height. Footer author name in Messina Sans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in Messina Sans Book #A6A6A6. No shapes, lines, borders or shadows.

### Slide 65

**Text**

And generative AI might be
on a path to that…
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
65
Computer Science > Computation and Language
arXiv:2303.12712 (cs)
[Submitted on 22 Mar 2023 (v1), last revised 13 Apr 2023 (this version, v5)]
Sparks of Artificial General Intelligence: Early experiments with GPT-4
Sébastien Bubeck, Varun Chandrasekaran, Ronen Eldan, Johannes Gehrke, Eric Horvitz, Ece Kamar, Peter Lee, Yin Tat Lee, Yuanzhi Li, Scott Lundberg, Harsha Nori, Hamid Palangi, Marco Tulio Ribeiro, Yi Zhang
Download PDF
Artificial intelligence (AI) researchers have been developing and refining large language models (LLMs) that exhibit remarkable capabilities across a variety of domains and tasks, challenging our understanding of learning and cognition. The latest model developed by OpenAI, GPT-4, was trained using an unprecedented scale of compute and data. In this paper, we report on our investigation of an early version of GPT-4, when it was still in active development by OpenAI. We contend that (this early version of) GPT-4 is part of a new cohort of LLMs (along with ChatGPT and Google's PaLM for example) that exhibit more general intelligence than previous AI models. We discuss the rising capabilities and implications of these models. We demonstrate that, beyond its mastery of language, GPT-4 can solve novel and difficult tasks that span mathematics, coding, vision, medicine, law, psychology and more, without needing any special prompting. Moreover, in all of these tasks, GPT-4's performance is strikingly close to human-level performance, and often vastly surpasses prior models such as ChatGPT. Given the breadth and

One photograph, a square AI-generated image (Midjourney) of a streamlined, teal-green vintage concept car parked on pale gravel in front of an ivy-covered wall. Behind the car are dense green ivy and shrubs, a tall pale stone or plaster pillar, and a white-framed window. The light is soft warm daylight from the upper left, giving a slightly soft, film-like look. Dominant colours are sage and olive green, teal-green car paint, beige gravel and warm brass. ### Slide 70

**Text**

(Maybe people don’t have / ‘general intelligence’ / either?) / WIKIPEDIA / The Free Encyclopedia / List of cognitive biases / 14 languages / Article / Talk / Tools / From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia / For common errors in logic, see List of fallacies. / Cognitive biases are systematic patterns of deviation from norm and/or rationality in judgment. They are often studied in psychology, sociology and behavioral economics. [1] / Although the reality of most of these biases is confirmed by reproducible research,[2][3] there are often controversies about how to classify these biases or how to explain them.[4] Several theoretical causes are known for some cognitive biases, which provides a classification of biases by their common generative mechanism (such as noisy information-processing[5]). Gerd Gigerenzer has criticized the framing of cognitive biases as errors in judgment, and favors interpreting them as arising from rational deviations from logical thought.[6] / Explanations include information-processing rules (i.e., mental shortcuts), called heuristics, that the brain uses to produce decisions or judgments. Biases have a variety of forms and appear as cognitive ("cold") bias, such as mental noise,[5] or / en.wikipedia.org / Benedict Evans –– December 2023 / 70

### Slide 71

**Text**

What are we measuring?
Is this intelligence? Reasoning?
Or information retrieval and pattern matching?
Or - the ability to look like people?
Should we be benchmarking against things that people are bad at? Or against things that people are good at?
Source: OpenAI
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
71

**Layout**

Two-column split slide. The left column (about 0 to 50% of the width) holds the title and body text on a light grey field, left-aligned at about 4.6% from the left edge. The title sits at about 25.6% from the top, with the body block below it from about 34.7% to 67% of the height. The right column (50 to 100%) is white and holds one framed chart figure centred vertically, from about 6.5% to 88% of the height. The source line sits bottom-left at about 91% and the footer (author, date, slide number) sits bottom-right at about 91%, aligned to the baseline of the source line.

**Visuals**

One bar chart, a reproduced figure from the GPT-4 technical report, shown in a white card with a soft drop shadow. Chart title: 'Exam results (ordered by GPT-3.5 performance)'. Subtitle: 'Estimated percentile lower bound (among test takers)'. Y axis: percent, labelled 0% to 100% at 20% intervals, light grey gridlines at 20% steps, range 0 to 100. X axis: exam names, rotated about 60 degrees, with the axis title 'Exam' centred beneath. Legend top right: 'gpt-4' dark green square, 'gpt-4 (no vision)' light green square, 'gpt3.5' blue square. Each exam has overlaid bars: blue gpt3.5 bar in front, light green gpt-4 (no vision) and dark green gpt-4 behind. Values are estimates (the figure prints no numeric labels): 1 AP Calculus BC: blue about 0, light green top about 43. 2 AMC 12: blue about 4, light green to about 19, dark green top about 45. 3 Codeforces Rating: blue and top about 5. 4 AP English Literature: blue and top about 8. 5 AMC 10: blue and top about 10. 6 Uniform Bar Exam: blue about 10, light green top about 90. 7 AP English Language: blue and top about 14. 8 AP Chemistry: blue about 22, light green top about 71. 9 GRE Quantitative: blue about 25, light green to about 62, dark green top about 80. 10 AP Physics 2: blue about 30, light green top about 66. 11 USABO Semifinal 2020: blue about 31, light green top about 99. 12 AP Macroeconomics: blue about 33, light green top about 84. 13 AP Statistics: blue about 40, light green top about 85. 14 LSAT: blue about 40, light green to about 83, dark green top about 88. 15 GRE Writing: blue and top about 54. 16 AP Microeconomics: blue about 60, dark green top about 82. 17 AP Biology: blue about 62, light green top about 85. 18 GRE Verbal: blue about 63, light green to about 97, dark green top about 100. 19 AP World History: blue and top about 65. 20 SAT Math: blue about 70, light green top about 89. 21 AP US History: blue about 74, dark green top about 89. 22 AP US Government: blue about 77, light green top about 88. 23 AP Psychology: blue and top about 83. 24 AP Art History: blue about 86, top about 86. 25 SAT EBRW: blue about 87, light green top about 93. 26 AP Environmental Science: blue about 91, top about 91. Figure caption printed under the chart inside the image: 'Figure 4. GPT performance on academic and professional exams. In each case, we simulate the conditions and scoring of the real exam. Exams are ordered from low to high based on GPT-3.5 performance. GPT-4 outperforms GPT-3.5 on most exams tested. To be conservative we report the lower end of the range of percentiles, but this creates some artifacts on the AP exams which have very wide scoring bins. For example although GPT-4 attains the highest possible score on AP Biology (5/5), this is only shown in the plot as 85th percentile because 15 percent of test-takers achieve that score.' Chart colours: blue #3F3FFF, light green #7CEE7C, dark green #55DD44, axis and text black #000000 in a serif-like font for the caption, sans for the chart labels.

**Design**

Background: Left half solid light grey #F2F2F2 and right half solid white #FFFFFF. Two-tone split: a light grey left panel and a white right panel, with no accent colour beyond the chart's greens and blue. Title in a light-to-regular modern serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif) in mid grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height roughly 3.9%). Body text in MessinaModern-Book serif, #545454, about 3.0% of slide height, with generous spacing between paragraphs. Italic 'look like'. Source and footer in MessinaSans (a neo-grotesque sans) in #545454 for the source and #A6A6A6 for the footer, about 1.9% of slide height; author name in bold. Chart card has a white fill, no border, and a soft diffuse drop shadow below and around it. No rounded corners, no gradients.

### Slide 72

**Text**

We don’t know
The engineers on the Apollo Program knew the
distance to the moon and the rocket’s thrust…
But we don’t know what AGI is, nor how far away it
is, nor how to get there, nor how close large language
models are, nor how much better they can get
We don’t have a chart for AGI
So how do you, personally, prefer to think about risk?
Source: NASA
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
72

**Layout**

Two-column split slide. The left half (about 0–48% width) is a white panel holding a left-aligned text block starting at about 4.6% from the left edge; the title sits at about 25.6% from the top, with the body paragraphs below it and the source line near the bottom at about 90.9%. The right half (48.3–100% width) is a full-height photograph bleeding to the top, right and bottom edges. A footer row at about 91% height holds the author name, date and page number 72 in small grey type along the bottom of the left panel area and the page number at about 94–95% x.

**Visuals**

One photograph occupying the right half of the slide, full-bleed to top, right and bottom edges, cropped to 51.7% width by 100% height. Subject: a Saturn V rocket on its launch pad at night, with the white-and-black first-stage and upper-stage body, the USA flags and 'UNITED STATES' lettering visible on the body, and a tall dark lattice launch tower with red-orange service arms and many lit windows beside it. Lighting: artificial floodlights on the pad and tower against a dusky sky. Dominant colours: dark teal-black tower and clouds at the top, warm orange-brown sky near the horizon, white rocket body, touches of red and green. Framing: vertical, rocket centred slightly left, tower to its right, pad structures and lights along the bottom edge. Not boxed; no overlay or tint. No logos or icons other than the small flags on the rocket.

**Design**

Background is a very light grey field (#F2F2F2) with a white (#FFFFFF) left panel covering the left half. Title in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif-like modern book face, colour #545454, large, approximately 5.6% of slide height. Body text in MessinaModern-Book, serif, #545454, approximately 3.0% of slide height, regular weight, line spacing about 3.6% between lines of one paragraph and blank paragraph gaps between blocks. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular, sans-serif, #545454, about 1.9%. Footer in MessinaSans, #A6A6A6, about 1.9%; author name bold. No shapes, rules, corner radii, shadows or gradients.

74

**Layout**

Left-aligned title and subtitle start at about x 20.5% near the top (y 5.6%), with no left column and a wide empty left margin. Three equal white circles sit in one horizontal row across the middle of the slide at y 34.3% to 72.8%, evenly spaced at x 19.6%, 47.0% and 74.5%, with grey block arrows pointing left between them. The footer line sits bottom right at y 91.1%, ending near x 96%, with the page number at the far right.

**Visuals**

Three white circles of equal size (about 21.7% of slide width) with thin outlines. The left circle and middle circle have dark grey outlines (#545454); the right circle has a red outline (#E84C3D). Two solid light grey (#A7A7A7) block arrows point left, one between the left and middle circles and one between the middle and right circles; each arrow has a triangular head and a rectangular shaft. Text inside the circles: left circle reads Ideas from 2000 with a quotation; middle circle reads Ideas from 2010 with a list; right circle reads Ideas for 2030, with the line Crypto! Metaverse! struck through by a horizontal line, then Generative AI. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient or texture. Titles and body text are dark grey #545454 in a Messina Modern serif family: title in Regular at about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle in Regular at about 2.6%, circle headings in SemiBold at about 3.3%, circle body text in Book at about 3.0%. Footer is in Messina Sans, bold name and book date, at about 1.9% in light grey #A6A6A6. Circles are white (#FFFFFF) filled with 2px-style outlines (#545454, red #E84C3D on the right one), no shadows, perfectly round corners. Arrows are flat #A7A7A7 with no outline or shadow. Red accent appears only on the outline of the 2030 circle.

**Layout**

A white chart panel fills the middle band with the chart title at the top left of the panel, a legend at the far left (about 3% to 13% width), and a bar chart whose plot area spans roughly 20% to 95% of the width. The bottom band is light grey, holding the source line at left and the footer 'Benedict Evans –– December 2023' with page number 75 at bottom right, all aligned to the same left margin (about 20.5%) and right margin (about 95%).

**Visuals**

Chart: clustered column chart (vertical bars, two series, positive revenue bars above zero and negative operating income bars below zero). Title: 'Meta ‘Reality Labs’ quarterly financials ($bn)'. Y-axis: labelled in $bn, range -5 to 1, gridlines at each whole unit, labels 1, 0, -1, -2, -3, -4, -5. X-axis: quarters Mar-21, Jun-21, Sep-21, Dec-21, Mar-22, Jun-22, Sep-22, Dec-22, Mar-23, Jun-23, Sep-23 (no values printed on bars). Series 1 Revenue (dark grey #545454), about values: Mar-21 about 0.5, Jun-21 about 0.3, Sep-21 about 0.5, Dec-21 about 0.85, Mar-22 about 0.7, Jun-22 about 0.45, Sep-22 about 0.3, Dec-22 about 0.75, Mar-23 about 0.35, Jun-23 about 0.25, Sep-23 about 0.2. Legend: at left, square swatch dark grey labelled Revenue above square red labelled Operating income. No highlighted points. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines; the zero baseline is a solid grey axis line at -5 along the bottom.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart panel. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454 serif (Messina Modern style, regular weight, roman). Chart title and legend in dark grey #545454 sans-serif (Messina Sans style). Accent colours: revenue dark grey #545454 and operating income red #EB483F. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart title about 2.2%, axis and legend text about 1.85%. Flat design: no shadows, gradients, rounded corners or borders on shapes; bars are plain rectangles with no outlines.

### Slide 76

**Text**

And all the externalities
Adding ‘AI’ to the spread of complex and conflicting regulatory questions…
‘Regulate big / tech!’
Privacy
Competition
Harmful / content
Encryption
AI Bias
Intellectual / property, / liability, / patents…
Geopolitics
Semi- / conductors
And more…
Benedict Evans –– December 2023 76

**Layout**

Title and subtitle are left-aligned at the top, starting around x 20.5% and sitting above the content. On the left, a red circle sits centred at roughly x 30%, y 50%, followed by a grey right-pointing arrow at x 46.5%, leading into a 3-column by 3-row grid of white tiles on the right (x 57.6% to 94.9%, y 20.9% to 86%). The footer sits bottom-right at y 91%: bold 'Benedict Evans', then '–– December 2023', then slide number 76 at the far right.

**Visuals**

A solid red circle (#EB483F), about 20.7% wide by 36.8% tall, with the text '‘Regulate big tech!’' in white serif centred inside it. To its right, a solid grey (#A7A7A7) right-pointing block arrow, about 5.2% wide by 9.3% tall, with a rectangular shaft on its left and a triangular head on its right, pointing toward the grid. On the right, a 3x3 grid of square-cornered white (#FFFFFF) tiles, each about 12.5% wide by 21.7% tall, separated by light grey gutters on a #F1F1F1 panel. Tile contents in reading order: row 1 'Privacy', 'Competition', 'Harmful content'; row 2 'Encryption', 'AI Bias', 'Intellectual property, liability, patents…'; row 3 'Geopolitics', 'Semi-conductors', 'And more…'. The bottom-right tile is a very slightly darker grey (#F1F1F1) than the other white tiles, so it reads as a recessed cell.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Light grey background (#F1F1F1). Titles and body text are dark grey (#545454) in a Messina Modern serif, regular weight. Footer text is light grey (#A6A6A6) in Messina Sans, with 'Benedict Evans' in bold. Title is large, about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle and grid text about 2.6% to 3.0%. The red circle (#EB483F) carries white serif text. Shapes are flat with square corners and no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 77

**Text**

Meanwhile, “back to the trend line”
But a trend line at 15-20% and growing!
Ecommerce as % US retail
Addressable retail*
Retail
25%
20%
15%
10%
5%
0%
Mar-00
Mar-05
Mar-10
Mar-15
Mar-20
Sep-23
Source: US Census. Seasonally adjusted
* Excluding cars, car parts & fuel
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
77

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top to y 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle left-aligned at x 20.5%, a white middle band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a light grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%) holding the source notes at left and the author, date and page number at right. The title sits at the top left of the header, the chart is left-aligned with the title at x 20.5%, the legend sits to the left at x 4.8%, and the right edge of the chart and footer is near x 96%. The footer source lines start at x 20.5% and the page number is at the far right, x 94.4%, with no logo.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled 'Ecommerce as % US retail', with the y axis from 0% to 25% in 5% steps (gridlines at each step, drawn as light grey dotted horizontal lines) and the x axis from Mar-00 to Sep-23 with labelled ticks at Mar-00, Mar-05, Mar-10, Mar-15, Mar-20 and Sep-23 (monthly data). Series 1, 'Addressable retail*', is a dark grey (#545454) solid line; it starts at about 1.2% in Mar-00, rises to about 5.5% by Mar-08, dips to about 5.2% around Mar-09, reaches about 10% around Mar-14, about 15% around Mar-19, spikes to about 22.8% around Mar-20, falls to about 21.2%, then holds about 21.5% to 21.7% through 2021-2022 and ends at about 22.6% in Sep-23. Series 2, 'Retail', is a red (#E8453C) solid line; it starts at about 0.7% in Mar-00, reaches about 3.7% around Mar-09, about 7.5% around Mar-16, about 10% around Mar-19, spikes to about 16.5% around Mar-20, falls to about 14.9%, stays near 14.6% to 14.8% through 2022, and ends at about 15.5% in Sep-23. Two thin grey dotted trend lines run across the chart, one for each series, rising from about 1% to about 23% (addressable) and from about 0.7% to about 15.5% (retail). Legend at upper left of the plot: a short dark grey line segment followed by 'Addressable retail*' and a short red line segment followed by 'Retail', stacked vertically. Highlighted points: the Mar-20 peaks of both series are the most prominent features, with no markers or data labels printed.

**Design**

Background: Full-slide #F1F1F1 light grey, with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle (y 20.5% to 86.1%). Flat, minimal style with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1 and the chart band is pure white #FFFFFF. Title text is #545454 in a serif (MessinaModern-Regular, regular weight, roughly 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle is #545454 in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart and axis labels are #545454 in a sans-serif (MessinaSans-SemiBold for the chart title, MessinaSans-Regular for other labels) at about 1.85% to 2.2% of slide height. Accent colours: series 1 dark grey #545454 and series 2 red #E8453C. Gridlines are light grey dotted hairlines. Footer author name is bold #A6A6A6, date is book weight #A6A6A6, page number is book weight #A6A6A6, and the source and footnote are regular #545454 at about 1.85% of slide height.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 0, y 0, w 100, h 100 [#F1F1F1]: Full-bleed background fill covering the whole slide (Flat fill, no border, no shadow; header and footer bands are this colour.)
- shape at x 0, y 20.5, w 100, h 65.6 [#FFFFFF]: White middle chart band (Flat fill, full width, no border or shadow.)
- shape at x 2.6, y 39.5, w 8.3, h 9.3 [#FFFFFF]: White rectangle masking hidden text behind the legend area (Flat white fill, no border; sits above the hidden 'Untitled 1' and 'Untitled 2' text and below the legend text.)

### Slide 78

**Text**

From exciting to universal / There were less than 100m PCs on Earth in 1993 - now 5bn people are online / Global computer users (bn) / 6 / 5 / 4 / 2 / 1 / Apple / Mosaic / Macintosh / 0 / 1980 / 1990 / 2000 / 2010 / 2020 / 2023 / Source: Morgan Stanley, Gartner, ITU, Apple, Google, CNNIC, Benedict Evans / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 78

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide split into three horizontal bands: a light grey header band at top (title and subtitle, left-aligned at x 20.5%), a white chart panel in the middle (y 20.5% to 86.1%), and a light grey footer band at bottom holding the source line at left and author, date and page number at right. Content starts at x about 20.5% with the right edge of the chart near 96.5%; the chart occupies the full width of the white panel to the right of the left margin. The title sits at the top of the header band; the page number '78' sits at the far right of the footer, right-aligned near x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Chart title: 'Global computer users (bn)', left-aligned above the plot. Y axis: 0 to 6 in units of billions (bn), gridlines at 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 are dotted light grey; the 0 value sits on a solid thin grey baseline. X axis: years 1980 to 2023, with labels printed at 1980, 1990, 2000, 2010, 2020 and 2023. Series: one smooth red line with no printed values; values are estimated (about): about 0.00bn in 1980, about 0.02bn at Apple Macintosh (about 1984), about 0.09bn at Mosaic (about 1993), about 0.4bn in 2000, about 0.9bn in 2005, about 1.5bn in 2010, about 2.8bn in 2015, about 4.0bn in 2018, about 4.8bn in 2020, about 5.25bn at the end in 2023. Highlighted points: two solid red dots on the line, one labelled 'Apple Macintosh' and one labelled 'Mosaic', both annotated above the line in grey text. Legend: none.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 background, with a full-width white #FFFFFF panel between y 20.5% and 86.1%. Light grey header and footer bands (#F1F1F1) framing a pure white chart panel (#FFFFFF). Title in a serif face (Messina Modern Regular style, roman, not bold) at roughly 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title and axis labels in a sans-serif (Messina Sans style): chart title SemiBold about 2.2% of slide height, axis labels Regular about 1.85%, colour #545554. Footer source line Regular about 1.85% in #545454; author name Bold and date Book about 1.85% in #A6A6A6. Accent colour: red #EF4437 for the data line and the two point markers. Gridlines dotted light grey (about #BFBFBF, 0.5px). Baseline solid grey about #8C8C8C, 0.5px. Line stroke about 3px in red. No shadows, no gradients, no rounded corners, no borders on the bands.

### Slide 79

**Text**

The new normal / Over half of new relationships in the USA begin online / US heterosexual couples who met online, by year of meeting / 60% / 50% / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / 1995 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Source: Stanford/GfK / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 79

**Layout**

Full-width two-tone layout. A light grey header band (top 20.5% of the slide) holds the title and subtitle, both left-aligned to a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width. A white content panel spans the middle (y 20.5% to 86.1%) and holds the chart, which starts at the same left edge as the title and runs to about 95% of width. A light grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%) holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, all sharing a baseline near 91%. The page number sits at the right margin at about 94.3% of width.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Title above the plot: US heterosexual couples who met online, by year of meeting. Y axis: percentage of couples, linear, 0% to 60%, gridlines and labels every 10%. X axis: year of meeting, 1995 to about 2021, labels at 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020. Single series, smooth red line about 0.5% of slide height thick, colour about #E8483C. Estimated values (no values printed on the slide, so these are about): 1995 about 1.5%; 2000 about 9%; 2005 about 19%; 2008 about 19.5%; 2010 about 21%; 2012 about 23%; 2015 about 31%; 2018 about 37%; 2019 about 41%; 2020 about 48%; 2021 about 53%. No legend. No highlighted points. Gridlines are thin dotted mid-grey lines (about #A6A6A6) at each 10% level. The 0% baseline is a solid thin grey line (about #8C8C8C). The line ends at the top right near 53%, not at a labelled point.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel across the middle band. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands framing a white (#FFFFFF) content panel. Title text is dark grey #545454 in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern serif such as Didone-influenced book serif), regular weight, title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Chart title and axis labels are sans-serif (MessinaSans family): chart title SemiBold about 2.2% of slide height in #545554, axis labels Regular about 1.85% in #545554. **Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white horizontal band across the middle, running from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. The title sits top-left in the grey header at the same left edge as the subtitle and chart title (about 20.5% from left). The chart fills the white band from the y-axis labels at about 18% from left to the right margin at about 95.5%. The legend sits in a column at the far left (about 4.8% from left) outside the chart. The source line sits bottom-left at about 20.5% from left, and the footer credit, date and page number sit bottom-right in the grey footer, with the page number at about 94.3% from left. All text is left-aligned except the footer and axis labels.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Global retail revenue ($bn)'. Y axis: 0 to 30, in $bn, labelled at 0, 10, 20, 30, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at 10, 20 and 30 and a solid thin grey baseline at 0. X axis: years 2010 to 2022, labelled 2010, 2015, 2020, 2022, no tick labels between. Series H&M (dark grey line, about 2.5px wide, smoothed): about 10 in 2010, about 10 in 2011, about 11 in 2012, about 12 in 2013, about 14 in 2014, about 16.5 in 2015, about 17.5 in 2016, about 18 in 2017, about 19.3 in 2018, about 21.2 in 2019, about 17 in 2020, about 17.8 in 2021, about 20.4 in 2022. Series Zara (mid grey line, smoothed): about 8.7 in 2010, about 9.5 in 2011, about 11.2 in 2012, about 11.5 in 2013, about 12.5 in 2014, about 14.7 in 2015, about 16.5 in 2016, about 17.8 in 2017, about 18.3 in 2018, about 19.8 in 2019, about 14.4 in 2020, about 19 in 2021, about 24.7 in 2022. Series Shein (GMV) (red line, smoothed, starts about 2018): about 1.3 in 2018, about 2.5 in 2019, about 8.2 in 2020, about 20.5 in 2021, about 29.4 in 2022. Legend at left with short line keys: H&M dark grey, Zara light grey, Shein (GMV) red. No points are highlighted with markers; the lines cross near 2021.

Series colours: H&M #4D4D4D, Zara #A6A6A6, Shein #E8473A. Gridlines dotted light grey #BFBFBF. Lines are plain with no shadows, gradients or rounded shapes.

### Slide 81

**Text**

And whatever comes next
New models will keep coming, especially from China (and buying customers on Meta)
Global search interest (relative)
Shein
Temu
Jan 15
Jan 16
Jan 17
Jan 18
Jan 19
Jan 20
Jan 21
Jan 22
Jan 23
Source: Google Trends
Benedict Evans
–– December 2023
81

**Layout**

Two grey bands frame a white content area: a header band across the top 20.5% of the slide and a footer band across the bottom 13.9%. The title and subtitle sit in the header, left-aligned at x 20.5%, with a left margin of about 20.5% for the chart column and the source line; the legend sits outside that column at x 4.8%. The chart fills the white area from the chart title down to the x-axis labels. The footer holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at x 94.5% (right margin about 4.5%).

**Visuals**

One line chart titled 'Global search interest (relative)'. Type: line chart with two series, smoothed lines, no markers. Y-axis: unlabelled, no printed numeric scale or range; four horizontal dotted gridlines, evenly spaced, with a solid grey baseline at the bottom (no y-axis tick values printed). X-axis: time, labelled Jan 15, Jan 16, Jan 17, Jan 18, Jan 19, Jan 20, Jan 21, Jan 22, Jan 23 at equal spacing, one label per January; the labels mark the start of each year. Series 1, Shein (dark grey #545454): begins just after Jan 16 at a very low value (about 2 percent of axis height), rises gradually to about 15 percent by Jan 18, about 25 percent at mid 2019, a step up to about 65 percent around Jan 20 with a peak near 70 percent in mid 2020, dips to about 45 percent late 2020, recovers to about 75 percent in early 2022, dips to about 62 percent, peaks at about 98 percent near mid 2023 (the highest point on the chart, touching the top gridline), falls to about 75 percent in late 2023 and ends near 62 percent. Series 2, Temu (red #E8453C): begins at about Oct 2022 near zero, rises slowly to about 10 percent by early 2023, then steeply to about 55 percent by mid 2023 and ends near 70 percent of axis height at the right edge of the chart, the last point. No data labels, no printed values; the values above are estimates. Legend: top-left of the chart area, outside the column, two entries stacked vertically: a short dark grey line swatch (#545454) with 'Shein', and a short red line swatch (#E8453C) with 'Temu'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of height. Flat, minimal, editorial look. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1 and the content band is white #FFFFFF, with no borders, rules, shadows or gradients. Titles are set in a serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern serif) in dark grey #545454, regular weight; the title is about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle about 2.6%. Body, chart labels and footer use a geometric sans-serif (MessinaSans, Regular/SemiBold/Book, closest common sans-serif) in #545454 at about 1.85% of slide height; the chart title is SemiBold at about 2.2%. The author name is bold #A6A6A6 and the date and page number are book weight #A6A6A6. Accent colours: Temu red #E8453C; Shein uses the body grey #545454. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. The baseline is a thin solid grey line.

### Slide 82

**Text**

New questions / Ikea builds a flagship in Central London… / (Taking the old ‘Top Shop’ fashion retailer’s space) / Is this logistics, retailing, marketing or advertising? / Source: Benedict Evans / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 82

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: a light grey text panel fills the left 43.7% of the slide and a full-height, full-bleed photograph fills the right 56.3%. The title 'New questions' sits at the upper-middle of the left panel, left-aligned at about 4.6% from the left edge, with three body lines stacked beneath it. The source line sits at the bottom left, and a footer row of author name, date and slide number runs along the bottom edge of the text panel at about 91% height.

**Visuals**

One photograph occupying the right side. Subject: a street in Central London (Oxford Street area) with a large blue hoarding covering a building under renovation. The left part of the hoarding shows a printed white and grey line drawing of a classical stone facade with windows and columns, and a blue banner with yellow lettering hangs from the blue panel near its centre. A red construction crane projects in from the right, and a sunlit blue sky with white cumulus clouds fills the upper right. In the street, two red double-decker buses (one showing 'Willesden' on its destination blind), a black London taxi, a cyclist in a helmet, and many pedestrians are visible. A blue poster reading 'Something big is coming' is partly visible at lower left. Lighting is bright daylight with strong sun on the facades. Dominant colours: vivid blue, red, white and grey, with black hoarding base and beige stone buildings. The photo is full-bleed, running to the top, bottom and right edges of the slide, with a hard vertical left edge at 43.7%. No overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 on the left panel, with the full-bleed photograph covering the right half. Left panel background #F2F2F2 with the photograph on the right half. Title in MessinaModern-Regular (a serif with slab-like features), dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Body lines in MessinaModern-Book serif, #545454, about 3.0% of slide height, with generous spacing between the three lines. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif, #545454, about 1.85% of slide height. Footer author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6 and date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85% of slide height. No shapes, rules, shadows or gradients on the text panel.

### Slide 84

**Text**

The last time this happened / First 50 years: what’s a car? What’s a car company? / Second 50 years: how do things change when / everyone has a car? / What new kinds of company and business are / created? / Source: Walmart / Benedict Evans / –– December 2023 / 84

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: the left half (0 to 50% width) is a light grey panel holding the title and three body paragraphs, left-aligned at x 4.6% with the source line anchored bottom-left. The right half is white and holds a single framed photograph of a 1979 annual report cover, centred in the right column, with the footer line (author, date, page number) bottom-right at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

One photograph: the cover of the Wal-Mart Stores, Inc. Annual Report 1979, shown upright as a portrait rectangle with a soft drop shadow, not cropped and not full-bleed. The cover has a diagonal gradient from orange-red at the top-left through yellow and teal-green to deep navy-blue at the bottom-right. Black bold sans-serif headline text reads 'Wal-Mart Stores, Inc. Annual Report 1979' across the top. The lower-right area shows a white-outlined step bar chart rising from left to right, with bars labelled by year and value: 1972 (bar with no legible value, lowest, about 0), 1973 125, 1974 168, 1975 236, 1976 340, 1977 479, 1978 678, 1979 900. Small black text at lower-left reads 'NET SALES / in millions of dollars'. Lighting is flat and even; the image shows print texture and scratches. Dominant colours: orange #E8602C, yellow #D9D12A, teal #1E7A72, navy #242F6B.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half and white #FFFFFF on the right half. Title is set in a serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight) in dark grey #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Body text is MessinaModern-Book serif, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 2.96% of slide height, with generous paragraph spacing. Source line and footer use MessinaSans sans-serif in grey #545454 (source) and #A6A6A6 (footer), about 1.85% of slide height; author name is bold. Shapes: the photo has a soft grey drop shadow and no border, square corners. No gradients on the slide background.

[05:16] it's exciting and you want to get a job in it and then there's a period when it becomes boring and that's where smartphones are now the new iPhone is amazing but it's not as exciting as the first iPhone it's become kind of a boring mature industry um it's gone through that cycle I think you could say that machine learning that we got excited about 10 years ago is now kind of most of the way through that cycle it's still a bit exciting but it's well on the way to being kind of a mature well understood technology that just goes into the deployment phase um and then generative machine learning is kind of at the beginning of that cycle we are wondering how it's useful but we're also very excited about it and I think to understand how that cycle progresses it's really useful to remember how we were talking about machine learning in 2013 when this really started working um so some people people in the room will know that machine learning is is really something that starts in the 1980s in fact it was kind of a dumb idea from the 80s that had never worked kind of like VR um and then in 2013 with imet this starts working and I would show demos like this to big companies and they would say uh well done that's very clever we're happy for you um like why
